# 项目: schema_semantic_constrained_ie

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-16980

## 输出风格

&lt;/project_context>
## Task: Implement Trie-Based GCD-Equivalent Constrained Decoding Baseline

### Background
We are writing a paper about OntoCoDec, a two-layer constrained decoding system for Information Extraction. Reviewers keep asking for direct comparison with GCD (Grammar-Constrained Decoding). GCD's core mechanism = vocabulary membership enforcement via trie-based prefix matching during autoregressive generation.

Our existing system has a "grammar-only" ablation that uses XGrammar to enforce JSON structure + vocabulary membership. We need to also implement a standalone trie-based constraint (without JSON structural constraints) to show that trie-only vocabulary membership ≈ GCD.

### What to Do

**Phase 1: Code Exploration (READ ONLY)**
1. SSH read-only to `westd-21461` and explore the project at `/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/`
2. Understand the existing constrained decoding pipeline:
   - How inference is run (main inference script, arguments)
   - How constraints are applied (grammar layer, semantic layer)
   - Where SciERC schema is defined (entity types, relation types)
   - How evaluation works (eval script, metrics: DR, CF, ET, RV, R-F1, E-F1)
   - Where model checkpoints are stored (LoRA checkpoints for Qwen and Llama on SciERC)
3. Document findings in a brief report.

**Phase 2: Implementation**
Implement a trie-based constrained decoding mechanism:
1. Extract all valid entity type names and relation type names from SciERC schema
2. Build a trie data structure where each path from root to leaf = a valid type/relation token sequence (after tokenization)
3. During decoding, when the model generates type/relation tokens, mask logits to only allow valid trie prefix continuations
4. Non-type/relation positions: unconstrained (no JSON structure enforcement)
5. The key difference from grammar-only: trie constrains ONLY the type/relation vocabulary tokens, NOT JSON structure. Grammar-only constrains BOTH via XGrammar.

Implementation approach:
- Create a new script or module (e.g., `trie_baseline.py`) that:
  - Takes the same input as the existing inference script
  - Builds the trie from schema
  - Runs inference with trie-only constraints
  - Outputs predictions in the same format for evaluation
- Reuse existing model loading, data loading, and evaluation code as much as possible
- The trie constraint should be tokenizer-aware (build trie from tokenized type names)

**Phase 3: Run Experiments**
Run 4 configs on SciERC (551 sentences):
1. Qwen3-8B + LoRA (FT) on SciERC — use existing checkpoint
2. Qwen3-8B zero-shot (ZS)
3. Llama-3.1-8B + LoRA (FT) on SciERC — use existing checkpoint
4. Llama-3.1-8B zero-shot (ZS)

Use GPU: `westd-21461 cuda:1` (cuda:0 is running a beam experiment, DO NOT use it)
Set `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1` for all runs.

Evaluate each config with the standard eval script, collecting: DR%, CF%, ET%, RV%, R-F1, E-F1.

**Phase 4: Results Collection**
Write results to `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/r16_trie_baseline_results.json` with structure:
```json
{
  "configs": {
    "qwen_ft": {"DR": ..., "CF": ..., "ET": ..., "RV": ..., "R_F1": ..., "E_F1": ...},
    "qwen_zs": {...},
    "llama_ft": {...},
    "llama_zs": {...}
  },
  "comparison": {
    "grammar_only": {"source": "Table 1 / existing results"},
    "full_s1": {"source": "Table 1 / existing results"}
  },
  "trie_construction": {
    "entity_types": [...],
    "relation_types": [...],
    "trie_size": ...
  }
}
```

### Key Constraints
- Use ONLY westd-21461 cuda:1 (CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1)
- Do NOT modify existing inference code — create new files
- Reuse existing evaluation pipeline
- If implementation is complex, start with Qwen FT as the first config to validate the approach works, then run the rest

### Server Info
- Server: westd-21461
- Project path: /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/
- Python environment: use whatever environment the project already uses (check if conda or venv)

- no constraints (baseline)\\n  grammar_only   - XGrammar JSON/enum structure enforcement\\n  semantic_only  - TypeConstraintProcessor domain/range masking\\n  full_s1        - both layers, compiler.type_consensus_processor import TypeConsensusProcessor\\n\\n\\nclass VocabTrimProcessor:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Mask tokens beyond XGrammar's vocab_size to prevent accept_token failures.\\n    \\n    Qwen3 has added special tokens (e.g. &lt;/think> at id 151668) beyond vocab_size (151643).\\n    XGrammar's bitmask only covers [0, vocab_size), leaving these tokens unmasked.\\n Also store original case\\n    name_to_pcode[name.strip()] = pcode\\n\\nVALID_RELATION_NAMES = set(name.lower().strip() for name in RELATION_TYPES.values())\\n\\n# Build valid pairs lookup: P-code -> set of (head_type, tail_type)\\nvalid_pairs_by_pcode = {}\\nfor pcode, constraint in RELATION_CONSTRAINTS.items():\\n    valid_pairs_by_pcode[pcode] = set(tuple(p) for p in constraint[\\\"valid_pairs\\\"])\\n\\ntotal_samples = 0\\nparse_failures = 0\\ntotal_entities_pred = 0\\ntotal_relations_pred = 0\\n\\n# Violation counters\\net_violations = 0  # entity type not in schema\\nrv_violations = 0  # relation name not in schema vocabulary\\ndr_violations = 0  # domain-range pair not valid\\n\\n# For F1\\nentity_tp = 0\\nentity_fp = 0\\nentity_fn = 0\\nrelation_tp = 0\\nrelation_fp = 0\\nrelation_fn = 0\\n\\nwith open(PRED_FILE) as f:\\n    for line in f:\\n        total_samples += 1\\n        doc = json.loads(line)\\n        \\n        parsed = doc.get(\\\"parsed_output\\\")\\n        gold_entities = doc.get(\\\"gold_entities\\\", [])\\n        gold_relations = doc.get(\\\"gold_relations\\\", [])\\n        \\n        if parsed is None:\\n            parse_failures += 1\\n            # All gold counts as FN\\n            entity_fn += len(set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in gold_entities))\\n            relation_fn += len(set((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], r[\\\"type\\\"]) for r in gold_relations))\\n            continue\\n        \\n        pred_entities = parsed.get(\\\"entities\\\", [])\\n        pred_relations = parsed.get(\\\"relations\\\", [])\\n        \\n        total_entities_pred += len(pred_entities)\\n        total_relations_pred += len(pred_relations)\\n        \\n        # --- Entity Type Violations ---\\n        for ent in pred_entities:\\n            if ent.get(\\\"type\\\") not in VALID_ENTITY_TYPES:\\n                et_violations += 1\\n        \\n        # --- Relation Violations ---\\n        for rel in pred_relations:\\n            rel_name = rel.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\").lower().strip()\\n            head_type = rel.get(\\\"head_type\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n            tail_type = rel.get(\\\"tail_type\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n            \\n            # RV: relation name not in vocabulary\\n            if rel_name not in VALID_RELATION_NAMES:\\n                rv_violations += 1\\n                # Can't check DR if relation unknown\\n                continue\\n            \\n            # DR: domain-range violation\\n            pcode = name_to_pcode.get(rel_name)\\n            if pcode and pcode in valid_pairs_by_pcode:\\n                if (head_type, tail_type) not in valid_pairs_by_pcode[pcode]:\\n                    dr_violations += 1\\n        \\n        # --- Entity F1 ---\\n        gold_ent_set = set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in gold_entities)\\n        pred_ent_set = set((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in pred_entities)\\n        \\n        entity_tp += len(gold_ent_set & pred_ent_set)\\n        entity_fp += len(pred_ent_set - gold_ent_set)\\n        entity_fn += len(gold_ent_set - pred_ent_set)\\n        \\n        # --- Relation F1 ---\\n        # Gold uses P-codes, pred uses names. Normalize gold to names.\\n        gold_rel_set = set()\\n        for r in gold_relations:\\n            rtype_pcode = r[\\\"type\\\"]\\n            rtype_name = RELATION_TYPES.get(rtype_pcode, rtype_pcode).lower().strip()\\n            gold_rel_set.add((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], rtype_name))\\n        \\n        pred_rel_set = set()\\n        for r in pred_relations:\\n            rtype_name = r.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\").lower().strip()\\n            pred_rel_set.add((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], rtype_name))\\n        \\n        relation_tp += len(gold_rel_set & pred_rel_set)\\n        relation_fp += len(pred_rel_set - gold_rel_set)\\n        relation_fn += len(gold_rel_set - pred_rel_set)\\n\\n# Compute metrics\\ndef prf(tp, fp, fn):\\n    p = tp / (tp + fp) * 100 if (tp + fp) > 0 else 0.0\\n    r = tp / (tp + fn) * 100 if (tp + fn) > 0 else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * p * r / (p + r) if (p + r) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return p, r, f1\\n\\ne_p, e_r, e_f1 = prf(entity_tp, entity_fp, entity_fn)\\nr_p, r_r, r_f1 = prf(relation_tp, relation_fp, relation_fn)\\n\\nparse_fail_rate = parse_failures / total_samples * 100 if total_samples > 0 else 0\\net_pct = et_violations / total_entities_pred * 100 if total_entities_pred > 0 else 0\\nrv_pct = rv_violations / total_relations_pred * 100 if total_relations_pred > 0 else 0\\ndr_pct = dr_violations / total_relations_pred * 100 if total_relations_pred > 0 else 0\\n\\nprint(f\\\"ET_pct: {et_pct:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"RV_pct: {rv_pct:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"DR_pct: {dr_pct:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"E_F1: {e_f1:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"E_P: {e_p:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"E_R: {e_r:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"R_F1: {r_f1:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"R_P: {r_p:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"R_R: {r_r:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"parse_fail_rate: {parse_fail_rate:.2f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"total_samples: {total_samples}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"total_entities_pred: {total_entities_pred}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"total_relations_pred: {total_relations_pred}\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(f\\\"--- Debug ---\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"parse_failures: {parse_failures}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"et_violations: {et_violations}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"rv_violations: {rv_violations}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"dr_violations: {dr_violations}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"entity_tp={entity_tp} fp={entity_fp} fn={entity_fn}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"relation_tp={relation_tp} fp={relation_fp} fn={relation_fn}\\\")\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.49}}"}

json.JSONDecodeError:\n        pass\n    cleaned = re.sub(r'^```(?:json)?\\s*', '', text.strip())\n    cleaned = re.sub(r'\\s*```$', '', cleaned)\n    try:\n        result = json.loads(cleaned)\n        if isinstance(result, dict):\n            return result\n    except json.JSONDecodeError:\n        pass\n    m = re.search(r'\\{[\\s\\S]*\\}', cleaned)\n    if m:\n        try:\n            return json.loads(m.group(0))\n        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n            pass\n    return None\n\n\ndef _chat_template_kwargs(tokenizer):\n    name = getattr(tokenizer, 'name_or_path', '').lower()\n    if 'qwen' in name:\n        return {'enable_thinking': False}\n    return {}\n\n\ndef build_messages(sample):\n    return [\n        {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": SCIERC_SYSTEM},\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": SCIERC_USER_TEMPLATE.format(text=sample[\"input_text\"])},\n    ]\n\n\ndef run_inference(model, tokenizer, sample, entity_trie, relation_trie,\n                  max_new_tokens, sampling_kwargs=None):\n    messages = build_messages(sample)\n    text = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\n        messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True,\n        **_chat_template_kwargs(tokenizer),\n    )\n    inputs = tokenizer(text, return_tensors=\"pt\").to(model.device)\n    prompt_len = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n\n    processor = TrieConstraintProcessor(\n        entity_trie, relation_trie, tokenizer, prompt_len)\n\n    gen_kwargs = dict(**inputs, max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens, do_sample=False,\n                      logits_processor=[processor])\n    if sampling_kwargs:\n        gen_kwargs.update(sampling_kwargs)\n\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        output_ids = model.generate(**gen_kwargs)\n\n    raw = tokenizer.decode(output_ids[0][prompt_len:],\n                           skip_special_tokens=True).strip()\n    return raw, parse_model_output(raw), processor.constraint_count\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Trie-based GCD-equivalent constrained decoding baseline\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--model_path\", required=True)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--data_path\", default=\"data/processed_data/test.json\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--output_path\", required=True)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--device\", default=\"cuda:0\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--max_new_tokens\", type=int, default=2048)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--max_samples\", type=int, default=0, help=\"0=all\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--seed\", type=int, default=42)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--repetition_penalty\", type=float, default=1.0)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--skip_on_error\", action=\"store_true\")\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    torch.manual_seed(args.seed)\n\n    print(f\"Mode: trie_only (GCD-equivalent)\")\n    print(f\"Model: {args.model_path}\")\n    print(f\"Device: {args.device}\")\n\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\n        args.model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        args.model_path, torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16, device_map=args.device,\n        trust_remote_code=True,\n    )\n    model.eval()\n\n    entity_trie = build_trie(ENTITY_TYPES, tokenizer)\n    relation_trie = build_trie(RELATION_TYPES, tokenizer)\n\n    print(f\"Entity trie: {len(ENTITY_TYPES)} types, {entity_trie.size} nodes\")\n    print(f\"Relation trie: {len(RELATION_TYPES)} types, {relation_trie.size} nodes\")\n\n    for name in ENTITY_TYPES:\n        ids = tokenizer.encode(name, add_special_tokens=False)\n        tokens = [tokenizer.decode([i]) for i in ids]\n        print(f\"  E '{name}': {ids} -> {tokens}\")\n    for name in RELATION_TYPES:\n        ids = tokenizer.encode(name, add_special_tokens=False)\n        tokens = [tokenizer.decode([i]) for i in ids]\n        print(f\"  R '{name}': {ids} -> {tokens}\")\n\n    samples = load_scierc_data(args.data_path)\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(samples)} samples\")\n    if args.max_samples > 0:\n        samples = samples[:args.max_samples]\n\n    sampling_kwargs = None\n    if args.repetition_penalty != 1.0:\n        sampling_kwargs = {\"repetition_penalty\": args.repetition_penalty}\n\n    Path(args.output_path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    n_ok, n_fail, total_constraints = 0, 0, 0\n\n    with open(args.output_path, \"w\") as fout:\n        for idx, sample in enumerate(samples):\n            try:\n                t0 = time.time()\n                raw, parsed, n_c = run_inference(\n                    model, tokenizer, sample, entity_trie, relation_trie,\n                    args.max_new_tokens, sampling_kwargs,\n                )\n                elapsed = time.time() - t0\n                total_constraints += n_c\n\n                if parsed is not None:\n                    n_ok += 1\n                else:\n                    n_fail += 1\n\n                if isinstance(parsed, list):\n                    parsed = {}\n\n                record = {\n                    \"doc_id\": sample[\"doc_id\"],\n                    \"input_text\": sample[\"input_text\"],\n                    \"gold_entities\": sample[\"gold_entities\"],\n                    \"gold_relations\": sample[\"gold_relations\"],\n                    \"raw_output\": raw,\n                    \"parsed_output\": parsed,\n                    \"inference_time_seconds\": round(elapsed, 3),\n                    \"mode\": \"trie_only\",\n                    \"decoding_strategy\": \"greedy\",\n                    \"num_beams\": 1,\n                }\n                fout.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\")\n                fout.flush()\n\n                n_ents = len(parsed.get(\"entities\", [])) if parsed else 0\n                n_rels = len(parsed.get(\"relations\", [])) if parsed else 0\n                status = \"OK\" if parsed else \"FAIL\"\n                print(f\"[{idx+1}/{len(samples)}] {sample['doc_id']}: {status} \"\n                      f\"E={n_ents} R={n_rels} C={n_c} {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n            except Exception as e:\n                if args.skip_on_error:\n                    n_fail += 1\n                    print(f\"[{idx+1}/{len(samples)}] {sample['doc_id']}: ERROR ({e})\")\n                    continue\n                raise\n\n    print(f\"\\nDone: {n_ok + n_fail} samples, success={n_ok}, fail={n_fail}\")\n    print(f\"Total trie constraints applied: {total_constraints}\")\n    print(f\"Output: {args.output_path}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYTHON_EOF\necho \"trie_baseline.py written\""
}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The output looks good:
1. Sample 1: Parsed successfully, entity types (Task, Material) and relation types (Used-for, Part-of) are all valid - trie constraint worked
2. Sample 2: Failed to parse because the model generated `"Part-of" or "Used-for"` (an ambiguous output) — this is expected without JSON structural constraints. The trie correctly constrained "Part-of" but the model then added "or" which broke the JSON.
3. Sample 3: Parsed successfully, all types valid.


The trie constraint forced the closing quote after "Part-of", but then the model continued generating ` or "Used-for"` — the trie only constrains vocabulary at the token level, not the overall JSON structure, so it couldn't prevent the model from continuing past where it should have stopped.

I'm also noticing that samples 1 and 3 are missing `head_type` and `tail_type` fields in their relations, while the violation checker expects these fields. Let me trace through how the checker handles missing fields...

Checking the violation checker code...

It uses `.get()` with a default empty string, so missing `head_type` or `tail_type` will be treated as empty values. This means domain/range violations will be inflated for the trie baseline since the model isn't consistently generating these fields. The entity type and relation vocabulary checks should still be accurate though.

Interestingly, the model does generate `head_type` and `tail_type` for sample 2 (despite the parsing failure), but omits them entirely for samples 1 and 3. This inconsistency suggests the fine-tuned model sometimes includes these fields and sometimes doesn't, which is exactly the kind of structural issue the trie constraint is meant to prevent.

Looking at the violation checker code...

If head_type and tail_type are missing, they'll be set to None, which means the domain-range check won't even run since it requires all three conditions (rel_match, head_type_match, and tail_type_match) to be true. So missing type fields won't trigger domain-range violations — they'll just skip that validation step entirely.

Now I'm checking how this affects the relation F1 computation, which uses a key function that extracts the head, tail, and normalized relation type from each relation object.

The dry run looks good and the output format matches what the evaluation pipeline expects. Before running the full experiment, I'm estimating the time requirements: with 551 samples across 4 different model configurations, that's over 2000 total runs. At roughly 2-3 seconds per sample, plus the overhead of loading each model sequentially, the entire experiment should take around 2 hours to complete.
```

  "command": "cat > /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/scripts/collect_r16_results.py << 'PYTHON_EOF'\n\"\"\"Collect trie baseline results into r16_trie_baseline_results.json.\"\"\"\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nOUTDIR = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs\")\nCONFIGS = {\n    \"qwen_ft\": \"r16_trie_qwen_ft_violations.json\",\n    \"qwen_zs\": \"r16_trie_qwen_zs_violations.json\",\n    \"llama_ft\": \"r16_trie_llama_ft_violations.json\",\n    \"llama_zs\": \"r16_trie_llama_zs_violations.json\",\n}\n\nENTITY_TYPES = [\"Task\", \"Method\", \"Material\", \"Metric\", \"OtherScientificTerm\", \"Generic\"]\nRELATION_TYPES = [\"Used-for\", \"Feature-of\", \"Hyponym-of\", \"Part-of\",\n                  \"Compare\", \"Conjunction\", \"Evaluate-for\"]\n\nresults = {\"configs\": {}, \"trie_construction\": {\n    \"entity_types\": ENTITY_TYPES,\n    \"relation_types\": RELATION_TYPES,\n}}\n\nfor config_name, filename in CONFIGS.items():\n    path = OUTDIR / filename\n    if not path.exists():\n        print(f\"MISSING: {path}\")\n        continue\n\n    with open(path) as f:\n        report = json.load(f)\n\n    s = report[\"summary\"]\n    q = report[\"quality\"]\n\n    dr_pct = round(s.get(\"domain_range_violation_rate\", 0) * 100, 2)\n    cf_pct = round(s.get(\"cross_field_consistency_violation_rate\", 0) * 100, 2)\n    et_pct = round(s.get(\"entity_type_violation_rate\", 0) * 100, 2)\n    rv_pct = round(s.get(\"relation_vocabulary_violation_rate\", 0) * 100, 2)\n    r_f1 = round(q[\"relation_f1\"][\"f1\"] * 100, 2)\n    e_f1 = round(q[\"entity_f1\"][\"f1\"] * 100, 2)\n\n    json_fail = s.get(\"json_parse_failure_rate\", 0)\n    json_fail_pct = round(json_fail * 100, 2) if json_fail <= 1 else round(json_fail, 2)\n\n    results[\"configs\"][config_name] = {\n        \"DR\": dr_pct, \"CF\": cf_pct, \"ET\": et_pct, \"RV\": rv_pct,\n        \"R_F1\": r_f1, \"E_F1\": e_f1, \"JSON_fail\": json_fail_pct,\n        \"total_samples\": s.get(\"total_samples\", 0),\n        \"total_entities\": s.get(\"total_predicted_entities\", 0),\n        \"total_relations\": s.get(\"total_predicted_relations\", 0),\n    }\n    print(f\"{config_name}: DR={dr_pct}% ET={et_pct}% RV={rv_pct}% CF={cf_pct}% \"\n          f\"R-F1={r_f1}% E-F1={e_f1}% JSON_fail={json_fail_pct}%\")\n\nout_path = OUTDIR / \"r16_trie_baseline_results.json\"\nwith open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n    json.dump(results, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\nprint(f\"\\nResults written to {out_path}\")\nPYTHON_EOF\necho \"collect script written\""
}
